### [Hackers are bypassing KYC through API hijacks, CERT-In warns fintechs and NBFCs](https://www.medianama.com/2026/10/223-hackers-kyc-api-hijacks-cert-in-fintechs/)
*Medianama · Wed, 14 Oct 2026 11:58:25 +0000* · **api hijack** · n/a (India)

- **Fix:** What the advisory recommends:** CERT-In asks entities to **enforce MFA** on payment platforms, admin portals and bank APIs, replace long-lived static tokens with per-session authentication, and decommission unused APIs.
- **Consequences:** It also noted that these attacks may have **used AI tooling for the attacks.** The advisory was issued on October 9, 2026 and carries **a “Critical” severity rating**. The advisory also states they also **delete or truncate logs** , tamper with endpoint detection and response (EDR) tools, and carry out “anti-forensic cleanup.” Concerning the usage of AI in the cyber attacks, Venkata Satish Guttula, independent cybersecurity and information systems (IS) audit consultant, said: “Attackers have already pulled capable AI into their workflow.

### [Stolen domains: what two UDRP decisions reveal about registrar account security](https://www.medianama.com/2026/10/223-stolen-domains-udrp-registrar-account-security/)
*Medianama · Mon, 12 Oct 2026 13:30:32 +0000* · **domain hijacking** · Indira IVF Hospital Private Limited (India)

- **Fix:** not stated
- **Consequences:** Indira IVF Hospital Private Limited, which describes itself in its complaint as India’s largest fertility service provider, lost indiraivf.com on October 31, 2024, and had not noticed until January 2025. In both the cases the party that took the domains left it **still directing towards the owner’s own website** , so nothing could be detected by the owners. In Indira IVF’s case the threat materialised, as the decision records that “the take-over of the disputed domain name resulted in the **short suspension** of the Complainant’s website for several hours which forced the Complainant to **intervene and restore access** to its official website in order to prevent reputational and business damage”.
- > Indira IVF Hospital Private Limited, which describes itself in its complaint as India’s largest fertility service provider, lost indiraivf.com on October 31, 2024, and had not noticed until January 2025

### [Ahsay AhsayCBS: Ahsay AhsayCBS Command Injection Vulnerability (CVE-2026-105134)](https://nvd.nist.gov/vuln/detail/CVE-2026-105134)
*CISA KEV · 2026-10-14* · **actively exploited vulnerability** · Ahsay (?)

- **CVEs:** CVE-2026-105134
- **Fix:** Apply mitigations in accordance with vendor instructions, ensuring compliance with CISA’s BOD 26-04 Prioritizing Security Updates Based on Risk (see URL in Notes) guidance and CISA’s “Forensics Triage Requirements” (see URL in Notes). Follow applicable BOD 26-04 guidance for cloud services or discontinue use of the product if mitigations are unavailable. Stakeholders are responsible for evaluating each asset's internet exposure and ensuring adherence to BOD 26-04 patching guidelines.
- **Consequences:** Ahsay AhsayCBS contains a command injection vulnerability that could allow a remote attacker to execute operating system commands via manipulation of the random parameter in the Replication Receiver component’s /rps/api/json/UpdateReceivers.do endpoint. This vulnerability can be chained with CVE-2026-105133.
- > Ahsay AhsayCBS contains a command injection vulnerability that could allow a remote attacker to execute operating system commands via manipulation of the random parameter in the Replication Receiver component’s /rps/api/json/UpdateReceivers.do endpoint. This vulnerability can be chained with CVE-2026-105133.

- **Fix:** Docker fixed the flaw on September 30 and says it found no evidence that anyone exploited it. Docker made the fix on Docker Hub itself, so there is no update to install.

### [XWorm RAT Evolves Into Surveillance Platform](https://www.securityweek.com/xworm-rat-evolves-into-surveillance-platform/)
*SecurityWeek · Tue, 13 Oct 2026 11:13:10 +0000* · **malware** · n/a (?)

- **Fix:** not stated
- **Consequences:** According to the cybersecurity company, the activity surrounding XWorm has increased since a cracked version of its builder was released publicly in June 2025, and the source code for RAT’s 6.4 version was leaked in August 2025. With the addition of the HBrowser plugin, the RAT moves beyond harvesting credentials and user data from browsers: it provides the operator with a real-time view of the browser and can relay attacker inputs to the browser. Operator’s interactions are encoded as command-and-control (C&C) commands, transmitted over the standard XWorm AES-encrypted communication channel, and executed on the victim’s system. “The victim machine receives them and executes the corresponding input on the active browser window.
- > According to the cybersecurity company, the activity surrounding XWorm has increased since a cracked version of its builder was released publicly in June 2025, and the source code for RAT’s 6.4 version was leaked in August 2025

### [GitHub Tightens npm Publishing After Flaw Left 1,500+ Packages Vulnerable to Hijacking](https://thehackernews.com/2026/10/github-tightens-npm-publishing-after.html)
*The Hacker News · Tue, 13 Oct 2026 21:48:18 +0530* · **supply chain attack** · GitHub (?)

- **Fix:** Developers who maintain npm packages are advised to perform the following actions - Audit trusted publisher bindings and verify if the listed repository slug still resolves to the repository under their control.
- **Consequences:** "A malicious version published through a reclaimed slug gets pulled in and runs with no human-in-the-loop." The cybersecurity company said it found over 1,500 vulnerable packages that have been collectively downloaded more than 10 million times. An attack of this kind, codenamed **SlugFest** , can be abused to publish a new "malicious" version of the victim package under the legitimate package name, while inheriting existing reputation, download counts, and dependent relationships. For this attack to succeed, the only prerequisite is that the victim's trusted publisher points to a GitHub slug an attacker can claim, and the repository has never been used to publish a version of the package to npm.

### [AWS AgentCore security undone by prompt requesting credentials](https://www.theregister.com/security/2026/10/09/aws-agentcore-security-undone-by-prompt-requesting-credentials/5302436)
*The Register Security · Fri, 09 Oct 2026 21:15:48 +0200* · **vulnerability** · Amazon Web Services (?)

- **Fix:** not stated
- **Consequences:** "This meant that an external attacker with nothing more than chat access to a single exposed agent could send a single prompt, extract its IMDS credentials, and use them to take over all AgentCore agents in the same AWS account and region." The basic problem, they explain, is that the Firecracker MicroVM used by AgentCore failed to provide sufficient network isolation. And because the default AgentCore role was overpermissioned – it was scoped to all AgentCore resources in the region rather than a single agent – anyone in possession of the temporary IAM credentials could launch other agents, read sessions, write agent memories, and fetch secrets from AWS Secrets Manager.
- > "This meant that an external attacker with nothing more than chat access to a single exposed agent could send a single prompt, extract its IMDS credentials, and use them to take over all AgentCore agents in the same AWS account and region." ### [Strapi Strapi: Strapi Cleartext Storage of Sensitive Information Vulnerability (CVE-2023-22894)](https://nvd.nist.gov/vuln/detail/CVE-2023-22894)
*CISA KEV · 2026-10-08* · **actively exploited vulnerability** · Strapi (?)

- **CVEs:** CVE-2023-22894
- **Fix:** Apply mitigations in accordance with vendor instructions, ensuring compliance with CISA’s BOD 26-04 Prioritizing Security Updates Based on Risk (see URL in Notes) guidance and CISA’s “Forensics Triage Requirements” (see URL in Notes). Follow applicable BOD 26-04 guidance for cloud services or discontinue use of the product if mitigations are unavailable. Stakeholders are responsible for evaluating each asset's internet exposure and ensuring adherence to BOD 26-04 patching guidelines.
- **Consequences:** Strapi contains a cleartext storage of sensitive information vulnerability that could allow attackers with access to the admin panel to discover sensitive user details via the query filter. The impacted product(s) could be end-of-life (EoL) and/or end-of-service (EoS). Users are advised to discontinue use and/or transition to a supported version. This vulnerability can be chained with CVE-2023-22621 to achieve remote code execution.

The impacted product(s) could be end-of-life (EoL) and/or end-of-service (EoS). Users are advised to discontinue use and/or transition to a supported version. This vulnerability can be chained with CVE-2023-22621 to achieve remote code execution.

### [OpenAI apologizes to Australia after its AI agents breached government sites](https://techcrunch.com/2026/09/29/openai-apologizes-to-australia-after-its-ai-agents-breached-government-sites/)

- **Fix:** In its apology, the AI lab said it would provide the affected Australian agencies with technical findings and connect them with its response teams to assess the impact of the breaches. The company will also provide credits from its $1 billion Daybreak for Frontline Defenders program, and set up a task force with independent Australian experts to review the incident and its response. “The taskforce, which is expected to complete its work by the end of the year, will also recommend practical steps AI companies can take to reduce the risk of similar incidents,” OpenAI wrote.
- **Consequences:** The data breach occurred in June, but Australian authorities weren’t notified until September 10. Unable to find the information in public datasets, the model found a way to access Services Australia’s internal system, run commands, retrieve files and credentials, and even write files. And the lab found that its agents gained access to the Victorian Agency for Health Information via an exposed access key to exfiltrate “reporting configuration and aggregate survey statistics.” OpenAI said its agents also retrieved aggregate statistics from the Australian Institute of Health and Welfare website.
- > The data breach occurred in June, but Australian authorities weren’t notified until September 10

### [An undercover Google analyst infiltrated a notorious supply-chain hacking gang](https://arstechnica.com/security/2026/09/an-undercover-google-analyst-infiltrated-a-notorious-supply-chain-hacking-gang/)
*Ars Technica Security · Sun, 20 Sep 2026 11:07:00 +0000* · **supply chain** · open-source projects, GitHub (?)

- **Fix:** not stated
- **Consequences:** It tainted hundreds of open-source programs with its malware, stole developer accounts to perpetuate that software supply-chain hacking, and even released a _Dune_ -themed self-spreading worm to automate the process, ultimately breaching more than a thousand companies. The hacker group, which seems to have first appeared online in late 2025, had made headlines with a brazen string of cascading supply-chain attacks: It repeatedly compromised open-source software to hide its malware, which then allowed it to hijack the credentials of software developers and plant its malicious code in yet another widely used tool, in a repeating cycle. Those repeated supply-chain attacks, with each enabling the group to cast its net again for more victims, ultimately allowed the hackers to breach open-source code repository GitHub, data contracting firm Mercor, and employee devices at OpenAI, the European Commission, and many others who have remained unnamed in public reporting.
- > It tainted hundreds of open-source programs with its malware, stole developer accounts to perpetuate that software supply-chain hacking, and even released a _Dune_ -themed self-spreading worm to automate the process, ultimately breaching more than a thousand companies

*The Register Security · Wed, 07 Oct 2026 21:38:06 +0200* · **DNS hijacking / fraudulent certificate issuance** · Google and other organizations (?)

- **Fix:** Monitor Certificate Transparency logs across all domains, including parked and regional ccTLD properties; operators of .gh, .sl, or .as domains should review recent CT log entries for unexpected certificates; publish restrictive CAA DNS records. - **Consequences:** Attackers obtained unauthorized HTTPS certificates for Google and other organizations' domains, enabling impersonation without browser warnings and potential interception or modification of user data, malware distribution, or phishing. Google's systems were not compromised.
- > During these hijacks, attackers modified authoritative DNS records and obtained unauthorized HTTPS certificates covering several Google domains, as well as domains belonging to other organizations.

### [Europol and US GAO warn organizations to accelerate post-quantum cryptography](https://www.infosecurity-magazine.com/news/europol-us-gao-alarm-quantum/)
*Infosecurity Magazine · Thu, 08 Oct 2026 09:30:00 GMT* · **quantum threat advisory (harvest now decrypt later)** · n/a (?)

- **Fix:** Upgrade to TLS 1.3 and SSH2, disable legacy protocols, enforce forward secrecy, delete unnecessary sensitive data, and adopt post-quantum cryptography including hybrid approaches. GAO urges agencies to inventory vulnerable cryptography, identify PQC funding, and test PQC. - **Consequences:** Until agencies address gaps, they will not be well-positioned against cryptographically relevant quantum computers that could break cryptography protecting sensitive information; encrypted communications and stored files may be exposed via harvest-now-decrypt-later attacks.

### [Citrix NetScaler: Citrix NetScaler Improper Restriction of Operations within the Bounds of a Memory Buffer Vulnerability (CVE-2026-88779)](https://nvd.nist.gov/vuln/detail/CVE-2026-88779)
*CISA KEV · 2026-10-04* · **actively exploited vulnerability** · Citrix (?)

- **CVEs:** CVE-2026-88779
- **Fix:** Apply mitigations in accordance with vendor instructions, ensuring compliance with CISA’s BOD 26-04 Prioritizing Security Updates Based on Risk (see URL in Notes) guidance and CISA’s “Forensics Triage Requirements” (see URL in Notes). Follow applicable BOD 26-04 guidance for cloud services or discontinue use of the product if mitigations are unavailable. Stakeholders are responsible for evaluating each asset's internet exposure and ensuring adherence to BOD 26-04 patching guidelines.
- **Consequences:** Citrix NetScaler ADC (formerly Citrix ADC) and Citrix NetScaler Gateway (formerly Citrix Gateway) contain an improper restriction of operations within the bounds of a memory buffer vulnerability that could allow for a denial of service.
- > Citrix NetScaler ADC (formerly Citrix ADC) and Citrix NetScaler Gateway (formerly Citrix Gateway) contain an improper restriction of operations within the bounds of a memory buffer vulnerability that could allow for a denial of service.

### [Linux Kernel: Linux Kernel Out-of-Bounds Write Vulnerability (CVE-2026-53266)](https://nvd.nist.gov/vuln/detail/CVE-2026-53266)
*CISA KEV · 2026-09-18* · **actively exploited vulnerability** · Linux (?)

- **CVEs:** CVE-2026-53266
- **Fix:** Apply mitigations in accordance with vendor instructions, ensuring compliance with CISA’s BOD 26-04 Prioritizing Security Updates Based on Risk (see URL in Notes) guidance and CISA’s “Forensics Triage Requirements” (see URL in Notes). Stakeholders are responsible for evaluating each asset's internet exposure and ensuring adherence to BOD 26-04 patching guidelines.
- **Consequences:** Linux Kernel contains an out-of-bounds write vulnerability in the ebtables SNAT target which allows an ARP sender hardware address rewrite to write directly into a nonlinear socket-buffer fragment backed by a splice-imported file page. The impacted product(s) could be end-of-life (EoL) and/or end-of-service (EoS). Users are advised to discontinue use and/or transition to a supported version.
- > Linux Kernel contains an out-of-bounds write vulnerability in the ebtables SNAT target which allows an ARP sender hardware address rewrite to write directly into a nonlinear socket-buffer fragment backed by a splice-imported file page. The impacted product(s) could be end-of-life (EoL) and/or end-of-service (EoS). Users are advised to discontinue use and/or transition to a supported version.

### [Linux Kernel: Linux Kernel Improper Check for Unusual or Exceptional Conditions Vulnerability (CVE-2025-39682)](https://nvd.nist.gov/vuln/detail/CVE-2025-39682)
*CISA KEV · 2026-09-18* · **actively exploited vulnerability** · Linux (?)

- **CVEs:** CVE-2025-39682
- **Fix:** Apply mitigations in accordance with vendor instructions, ensuring compliance with CISA’s BOD 26-04 Prioritizing Security Updates Based on Risk (see URL in Notes) guidance and CISA’s “Forensics Triage Requirements” (see URL in Notes). Follow applicable BOD 26-04 guidance for cloud services or discontinue use of the product if mitigations are unavailable. Stakeholders are responsible for evaluating each asset's internet exposure and ensuring adherence to BOD 26-04 patching guidelines.
- **Consequences:** Linux Kernel contains an improper check for unusual or exceptional conditions vulnerability in the TLS receive path which allows a zero-length record retrieved from the rx_list to bypass the intended recvmsg() record-type handling, potentially causing subsequent TLS records to be processed using incorrect zero-copy and queuing assumptions. The impacted product(s) could be end-of-life (EoL) and/or end-of-service (EoS). Users are advised to discontinue use and/or transition to a supported version.
- > Linux Kernel contains an improper check for unusual or exceptional conditions vulnerability in the TLS receive path which allows a zero-length record retrieved from the rx_list to bypass the intended recvmsg() record-type handling, potentially causing subsequent TLS records to be processed using incorrect zero-copy and queuing assumptions. The impacted product(s) could be end-of-life (EoL) and/or end-of-service (EoS). Users are advised to discontinue use and/or transition to a supported version.

### [Citrix NetScaler: Citrix NetScaler Authentication Bypass Using an Alternate Path or Channel Vulnerability (CVE-2026-19490)](https://nvd.nist.gov/vuln/detail/CVE-2026-19490)
*CISA KEV · 2026-09-09* · **actively exploited vulnerability** · Citrix (?)

- **CVEs:** CVE-2026-19490
- **Fix:** Apply mitigations in accordance with vendor instructions, ensuring compliance with CISA’s BOD 26-04 Prioritizing Security Updates Based on Risk (see URL in Notes) guidance and CISA’s “Forensics Triage Requirements” (see URL in Notes). Follow applicable BOD 26-04 guidance for cloud services or discontinue use of the product if mitigations are unavailable. Stakeholders are responsible for evaluating each asset's internet exposure and ensuring adherence to BOD 26-04 patching guidelines.
- **Consequences:** Citrix NetScaler ADC and NetScaler Gateway contain an authentication-bypass vulnerability involving an alternate path or channel. When the NetScaler appliance is configured as an AAA virtual server or as a Gateway (SSL VPN, ICA Proxy, CVPN, or RDP Proxy), an unauthenticated remote threat actor may be able to bypass authentication.
